  • NGC_KEY — NGC API key (container image pulls from nvcr.io; get it at NGC →)
  • HF_TOKEN — HuggingFace access token with read scope (nvidia/Cosmos-Reason2-8B is a gated model; accept the agreement at huggingface.co/nvidia/Cosmos-Reason2-8B first)
  • GOOGLE_API_KEY — Google Gemini API key (VLM annotation step in the data-generation loop; or use vlm.backend: "openai" with vlm.openai.api_key)
  • ACCESS_KEY + SECRET_KEY — S3-compatible credentials (only if workspace / dataset / results URIs use s3://)
Also export credentials for your target compute backend — see the matching Platform how-to.

Shared setup

Step 1 — Imports + SDK + timestamped workspace.

import os
from datetime import datetime
from tao_sdk.platforms.slurm import SlurmSDK
from tao_sdk.script_runner   import build_entrypoint

TIMESTAMP        = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
sdk              = SlurmSDK()
WORKSPACE_URI    = f"<workspace-uri>/cosmos-rl-deft/{TIMESTAMP}"
current_ckpt_uri = "<baseline-checkpoint-uri>"   # or None to train from scratch on the first iter

Per-Iteration Stages

Stage 1 — Gap analysis on the current Cosmos-RL model. Identifies the weakest video-QA buckets. Submit a TAO data-services job whose command runs your gap-analysis script over the current eval predictions; emit a Parquet file listing the weakest clips.

ep_gap = build_entrypoint(
    command="gap_analysis cosmos_rl --predictions /data/predictions.parquet --output /results/weak.parquet",
    inputs={"/data/predictions.parquet": "<current-predictions-uri>"},
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
gap = sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-data-services",
    command=ep_gap["command"],
    gpu_count=1,
)

Stage 2 — Caption new clips with Qwen. See How To Caption Images and Videos with Qwen VLM for the exact sdk.create_job(...) invocation — point its --weak-video-list at the parquet from Stage 1.

Stage 3 — Generate synthetic videos with Cosmos Predict 2.5. See How To Generate Synthetic Video with Cosmos Predict 2.5 — use the captions JSONL from Stage 2 as --input-json.

Stage 4 — Merge the new clips into the training set. A small data-prep job that updates the LLaVA-style annotation JSON to reference the new clips, ensuring every record has video_fps (Cosmos-RL requires it).

ep_merge = build_entrypoint(
    command="cosmos_rl merge --train-annotations /data/train/annotations.json"
            " --new-clips /data/new_clips --output /results/merged",
    inputs={
        "/data/train/annotations.json": "<current-train-annotations-uri>",
        "/data/new_clips":              "<generated-videos-uri>",
    },
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
merge = sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-data-services",
    command=ep_merge["command"],
    gpu_count=1,
)

Stage 5 — Fine-tune Cosmos-Reason2-8B on the merged dataset.Seed the job with the current checkpoint. See the Cosmos-RL model entry for the full default spec template and required overrides (custom.train_dataset.annotation_path / media_path, etc.).

ep_train = build_entrypoint(
    command="cosmos-rl --config /specs/train.toml /opt/cosmos_rl/tao_sft_example.py",
    inputs={
        "/specs/train.toml":               "<train-spec-uri-this-iter>",
        "/data/init_checkpoint":           current_ckpt_uri,
        "/data/train/annotations.json":    "<merged-annotations-uri>",
        "/data/train":                     "<merged-media-uri>",
        "/data/eval/annotations.json":     "<eval-annotations-uri>",
        "/data/eval":                      "<eval-media-uri>",
    },
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
train = sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-cosmos-rl",
    command=ep_train["command"],
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>, num_nodes=<num-nodes>,
    env_vars={"HF_TOKEN": os.environ["HF_TOKEN"]},
    partition="<partition>", account="<account>",
)

Wrap the Stages in a Python Loop

Run stages 1–5 each iteration, thread each iteration's best checkpoint into the next iteration's /data/init_checkpoint, and track the per-iteration metric.

for i in range(<num-iterations>):
    # Stage 1 — gap analysis
    # Stage 2 — qwen captioning
    # Stage 3 — cosmos-predict-2.5 generation
    # Stage 4 — merge
    # Stage 5 — fine-tune (above) — read its new checkpoint URI from outputs
    current_ckpt_uri = "<new-best-checkpoint-uri-this-iter>"

How To Run the Full AOI Improvement Loop workflow

Drive a Visual ChangeNet / ChangeNet PCB inspection model to a hard KPI (e.g. FAR < 0.1% at recall = 100%) by iterating baseline evaluate → RCA → synthetic defects → k-NN mining → retrain → deployment gating. There is no single SDK class — every stage is a separate sdk.create_job(...) call you submit per iteration. Use this loop only for the full KPI-driven improvement workflow; for standalone training, one-off inference, generic anomaly generation, or RCA-only analysis, use the corresponding dedicated how-to.

applicationworkflowdata-efficientaoilooppcb
Step 0 — Confirm environment variables (before anything else in this flow — even after collecting the action inputs). Collect every action input from the user — never pick one yourself. This covers the model / checkpoint (model_path), gpu_count, num_nodes, the compute backend, and every other <…> placeholder: prompt the user for each before composing the command, and where the guide names a default or recommended value (or you could infer one from the hardware, e.g. reading nvidia-smi to size gpu_count), offer it as a suggestion in that prompt but do not adopt it until the user confirms. This action uses the variables listed below: required ones must already be exported in the shell that launched your agent; optional ones are only needed for the cases noted. Before executing any later step, ask the user to confirm that every required variable below has been set — expect a plain "yes" or "no, let me set them and restart." Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. Defer any actual environment check to the moment just before you run the code that consumes these variables. If any required variable is missing, stop here — have the user export it in the launching shell and restart the agent before continuing; do not set it mid-session, since later steps read it from the launching environment.
  • NGC_KEY — NGC API key (container image pulls from nvcr.io; get it at NGC →)
  • ACCESS_KEY + SECRET_KEY — S3-compatible credentials (only if workspace / per-iteration dataset URIs use s3://)
  • HF_TOKEN — optional; only if the retrain step uses a gated HuggingFace backbone (e.g. Visual ChangeNet with C-RADIOv2-B)
Also export credentials for your target compute backend — see the matching Platform how-to.

Shared setup

Step 1 — Imports + SDK + KPI targets.

import os
from datetime import datetime
from tao_sdk.platforms.slurm import SlurmSDK
from tao_sdk.script_runner   import build_entrypoint

TIMESTAMP        = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
sdk              = SlurmSDK()
WORKSPACE_URI    = f"<workspace-uri>/aoi-deft/{TIMESTAMP}"
TARGET_RECALL    = <target-recall>    # e.g. 1.0
TARGET_FAR       = <target-far>       # e.g. 0.001
current_ckpt_uri = "<baseline-checkpoint-uri>"

Per-Iteration Stages

Stage 1 — Baseline / latest Visual ChangeNet evaluation. Submit an evaluate job for the current model (see Visual ChangeNet for the spec template). Read recall and FAR from the produced metrics file; if both meet the KPI, exit the loop.

Stage 2 — RCA on the latest experiment. See How To Run RCA on a ChangeNet Experiment for the exact sdk.create_job(...) shape — point it at the experiment directory from Stage 1.

Stage 3 — Generate synthetic defects with Cosmos AnomalyGen. See How To Run the Cosmos AnomalyGen Pipeline — feed it the failure clusters from Stage 2 as the training dataset.

Stage 4 — k-NN mining for real images similar to the failure cases. See How To Run k-NN Mining for VCN AOI Iteration.

Stage 5 — Retrain Visual ChangeNet on the augmented set. Seeded with the current iteration's best checkpoint. See Visual ChangeNet for the train-action spec.

ep_train = build_entrypoint(
    command="visual_changenet train -e /specs/train_classify.yaml",
    inputs={
        "/specs/train_classify.yaml":                  "<train-spec-uri-this-iter>",
        "/data/init_checkpoint.pth":                   current_ckpt_uri,
        "/data/train/images.tar.gz":                   "<augmented-train-images-uri>",
        "/data/train/dataset.csv":                     "<augmented-train-csv-uri>",
        "/data/eval/images.tar.gz":                    "<eval-images-uri>",
        "/data/eval/dataset.csv":                      "<eval-csv-uri>",
    },
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
train = sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-pyt",
    command=ep_train["command"],
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>, num_nodes=<num-nodes>,
    env_vars={"HF_TOKEN": os.environ.get("HF_TOKEN", "")},
    partition="<partition>", account="<account>",
)

Wrap the Stages in a Python Loop

Run stages 1–5 each iteration; thread each iteration's best checkpoint into the next iteration's /data/init_checkpoint.pth; stop when the KPI is met or <max-iterations> is reached.

for i in range(<max-iterations>):
    # Stage 1 — evaluate; parse recall + FAR
    recall, far = ...  # read from Stage 1's metrics
    if recall >= TARGET_RECALL and far <= TARGET_FAR:
        print(f"KPI met at iteration {i}")
        break

    # Stage 2 — RCA on the latest experiment
    # Stage 3 — Cosmos AnomalyGen synthetic defects
    # Stage 4 — k-NN mining
    # Stage 5 — retrain (above) — read its new checkpoint URI from outputs
    current_ckpt_uri = "<new-best-checkpoint-uri-this-iter>"

Models · Train, Evaluate, Infer, Export

Each model how-to below lists the container image, monitoring metric, and the command list to pass to sdk.create_job(...). The surrounding SDK boilerplate is identical for every model — see "The Common SDK Pattern" first, then copy a per-model command from any entry that follows.

Reminder — what each model entry shows. The specs block in every entry below is the set of overrides the user must supply (dataset URIs, num_classes, num_epochs, etc.) — not the complete spec. Each model entry contains a collapsed "Full default spec template" block — expand it, copy the full YAML, fill in your dataset paths and overrides, and use that as the base. See How To Construct the specs Dict for the load→override flow, and How To Use This Guide for the <…> placeholder convention.

The Common SDK Pattern read first

Every TAO job is a two-call pattern: build_entrypoint(...) wraps your action command with the S3/HF/NGC download + output upload runtime, then sdk.create_job(...) submits the wrapped command. The SDK signatures are:

build_entrypoint(*, command, inputs=None, outputs=None,
                 specs=None, args=None, config_format="toml",
                 upload_excludes=None, mount_path=None, flags=None) -> dict
sdk.create_job(image, command, gpu_count=1, env_vars=None, **kwargs) -> Job

Workflow for any TAO model action (train / evaluate / inference / export / gen_trt_engine):

  1. Edit the model's spec YAML. Each per-model entry below contains a collapsed "Full default spec template" block — copy that YAML, fill in your dataset paths and overrides (num_classes, num_epochs, num_gpus, …), and save it somewhere reachable by a URI (S3, Lustre, local file, …). Inside the spec, point file-valued keys (e.g. dataset.train_data_sources[0].image_dir) at container-side paths you pick — they must match the keys in inputs passed to build_entrypoint.
  2. Inline the staging runtime with build_entrypoint(...). Pass:
    • command — a string that runs the action and points at the spec file at its container path, e.g. "dino train -e /specs/train.yaml".
    • inputs — a path-keyed dict {container_path: uri}; each URI is downloaded to that container path before the command runs. Include the spec file itself plus every data file the spec references.
    • outputs — a list of container paths to upload after the command finishes (e.g. ["/results/"]).
  3. Submit via sdk.create_job(...). Pass image, command=ep["command"], gpu_count, env_vars, plus platform-specific kwargs (partition, account, namespace, instance_id, …).
import os
from tao_sdk.platforms.slurm import SlurmSDK   # or BrevSDK, KubernetesSDK, DockerSDK
from tao_sdk.script_runner   import build_entrypoint

sdk = SlurmSDK()                                  # SDK reads creds from env vars you exported earlier

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="<action-command>",                   # <-- copy from the chosen model entry (e.g. "dino train -e /specs/train.yaml")
    inputs={
        "/specs/train.yaml":                "<edited-spec-uri>",
        # one entry per file-valued spec key the action consumes — see the
        # per-model entry below for the exact set. Container paths are
        # arbitrary but MUST match what the spec YAML references.
        "/data/train/images.tar.gz":        "<train-images-uri>",
        "/data/train/annotations.json":     "<train-annotations-uri>",
        "/data/eval/images.tar.gz":         "<eval-images-uri>",
        "/data/eval/annotations.json":      "<eval-annotations-uri>",
    },
    outputs=["/results/"],                        # list of container paths to upload
)
job = sdk.create_job(
    image="<image>",                              # <-- copy from the chosen model entry's "Image" field
    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>, num_nodes=<num-nodes>,
    env_vars={"HF_TOKEN": os.environ.get("HF_TOKEN", "")},
    partition="<partition>", account="<account>", # platform-specific kwargs (SlurmSDK in this example)
)
print(sdk.get_job_status(job.id))
print(sdk.get_job_logs(job.id))

Switch action by changing the command string and (where the action consumes different files) the entries in inputs. TensorRT actions (gen_trt_engine, TRT evaluate, TRT inference) use the …:7.0.0-deploy image; everything else uses the network's primary image.

For path-driven scripts that don't read a spec file (data skills like image-grounding, qwen-caption, video-reasoning-annotation), the shape is identical — just drop the spec file from inputs and pass the CLI flags directly in command:

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="qwen-caption --input /data/images --output /results/captions.jsonl",
    inputs={"/data/images": "<input-images-uri>"},
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
sdk.create_job(
    image="<image>",
    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>,
)

From here on, each model and data entry below shows what is specific to it — the command string and which files the action consumes — together with the full default spec YAML you edit before submitting.

How To Train Mask2Former segmentation

Mask2Former — universal segmentation (panoptic, instance, semantic) with masked attention for high-quality masks.

segmentationpanopticinstancesemantic
Step 0 — Confirm environment variables (before anything else in this flow — even after collecting the action inputs). Collect every action input from the user — never pick one yourself. This covers the model / checkpoint (model_path), gpu_count, num_nodes, the compute backend, and every other <…> placeholder: prompt the user for each before composing the command, and where the guide names a default or recommended value (or you could infer one from the hardware, e.g. reading nvidia-smi to size gpu_count), offer it as a suggestion in that prompt but do not adopt it until the user confirms. This action uses the variables listed below: required ones must already be exported in the shell that launched your agent; optional ones are only needed for the cases noted. Before executing any later step, ask the user to confirm that every required variable below has been set — expect a plain "yes" or "no, let me set them and restart." Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. Defer any actual environment check to the moment just before you run the code that consumes these variables. If any required variable is missing, stop here — have the user export it in the launching shell and restart the agent before continuing; do not set it mid-session, since later steps read it from the launching environment.
  • NGC_KEY — NGC API key (container image pulls from nvcr.io; get it at NGC →)
  • ACCESS_KEY + SECRET_KEY — S3-compatible credentials (only if dataset / results URIs use s3://)
  • WANDB_API_KEY — optional; only if wandb.enable: true in your spec
Also export credentials for your target compute backend — see the matching Platform how-to.

pose6-dofkeypoints
Step 0 — Confirm environment variables (before anything else in this flow — even after collecting the action inputs). Collect every action input from the user — never pick one yourself. This covers the model / checkpoint (model_path), gpu_count, num_nodes, the compute backend, and every other <…> placeholder: prompt the user for each before composing the command, and where the guide names a default or recommended value (or you could infer one from the hardware, e.g. reading nvidia-smi to size gpu_count), offer it as a suggestion in that prompt but do not adopt it until the user confirms. This action uses the variables listed below: required ones must already be exported in the shell that launched your agent; optional ones are only needed for the cases noted. Before executing any later step, ask the user to confirm that every required variable below has been set — expect a plain "yes" or "no, let me set them and restart." Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. Defer any actual environment check to the moment just before you run the code that consumes these variables. If any required variable is missing, stop here — have the user export it in the launching shell and restart the agent before continuing; do not set it mid-session, since later steps read it from the launching environment.

OCRNet input file formats.
  • dataset.train_dataset_dir / dataset.val_dataset_dir — LMDB folders (each containing data.mdb and lock.mdb). Generate them with a ocrnet dataset_convert job first (see dataset_convert.input_img_dir + dataset_convert.gt_file inputs below). Alternatively, leave these empty and use the raw-folder path via dataset.train_gt_file / dataset.val_gt_file.
  • dataset.train_gt_file / dataset.val_gt_file — Tab-separated text file, one record per line: <relative_image_filename>\t<text_label> (e.g. img001.jpg\thello). Used only when pointing at raw image folders instead of LMDB.
  • dataset.character_list_file — Plain-text file with one character per line defining the recognition vocabulary (e.g. a, b, …, 0, …, 9). All characters present in training labels must appear in this file.
command = "ocrnet train -e /specs/train.yaml"
inputs = {
    "/specs/train.yaml":                "<edited-spec-uri>",  # paste the edited template; see <details> above
    "/data/train/train_dataset_dir":  "<train_dataset_dir-uri>",   # LMDB folder; run ocrnet dataset_convert to generate
    "/data/eval/val_dataset_dir":     "<val_dataset_dir-uri>",   # LMDB folder; run ocrnet dataset_convert to generate
    "/data/character_list.txt":       "<character_list_file-uri>",   # one character per line; spec key: dataset.character_list_file
}
outputs = ["/results/"]

How To Train PointPillars (LiDAR 3D) 3d detection

PointPillars — 3D object detection from LiDAR point clouds. Encodes points into a pseudo-image via pillar-based representation, then applies 2D detection.

3d-detectionlidarpoint-cloud
Step 0 — Confirm environment variables (before anything else in this flow — even after collecting the action inputs). Collect every action input from the user — never pick one yourself. This covers the model / checkpoint (model_path), gpu_count, num_nodes, the compute backend, and every other <…> placeholder: prompt the user for each before composing the command, and where the guide names a default or recommended value (or you could infer one from the hardware, e.g. reading nvidia-smi to size gpu_count), offer it as a suggestion in that prompt but do not adopt it until the user confirms. This action uses the variables listed below: required ones must already be exported in the shell that launched your agent; optional ones are only needed for the cases noted. Before executing any later step, ask the user to confirm that every required variable below has been set — expect a plain "yes" or "no, let me set them and restart." Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. Defer any actual environment check to the moment just before you run the code that consumes these variables. If any required variable is missing, stop here — have the user export it in the launching shell and restart the agent before continuing; do not set it mid-session, since later steps read it from the launching environment.
  • NGC_KEY — NGC API key (container image pulls from nvcr.io; get it at NGC →)
  • ACCESS_KEY + SECRET_KEY — S3-compatible credentials (only if dataset / results URIs use s3://)
Also export credentials for your target compute backend — see the matching Platform how-to.

How To Run NV-Tesseract AD Diffusion (time-series anomaly detection) anomaly detection

NV-Tesseract AD Diffusion — diffusion-based anomaly detection and fine-tuning for multivariate time series. The model reconstructs randomly masked segments and scores each timestep by MAE between reconstruction and original signal; adaptive thresholding (SCS or MACS) converts scores to binary labels. Runs standalone via uv — no TAO Execution SDK required.

anomaly-detectiontime-seriesdiffusionnv-tesseractfinetuneinference

Source: github.com/NVIDIA/NV-Tesseract · Weights: huggingface.co/nvidia/nv-tesseract-ad-diffusion · Runtime: Python 3.12+, uv · GPU: optional (CPU supported)

One-time setup:

pip install uv                          # install uv if not already present
git clone https://github.com/NVIDIA/NV-Tesseract.git
cd NV-Tesseract/ad_diffusion
uv sync                                 # install all Python dependencies

# Optional — only needed if the HF repo is gated
export HUGGINGFACE_HUB_TOKEN="hf_..."
# or: uv run huggingface-cli login

Inference (high-level helper — recommended): annotates your DataFrame with binary Anomaly labels and per-timestep MAE scores. Weights are auto-downloaded from HuggingFace on first run.

import sys, pandas as pd
sys.path.append("/path/to/NV-Tesseract/ad_diffusion")
from sdk.anomaly_analysis import perform_anomaly_analysis_with_diffusion

df = pd.read_csv("your_data.csv")    # numeric feature columns only; no NaN/\u00b1Inf

results = perform_anomaly_analysis_with_diffusion(
    df=df,
    threshold_strategy="scs",        # "scs" (fast) or "macs" (adaptive per-feature)
    model_path=None,                 # None \u2192 auto-download final_model.pth from HF
    config_path=None,                # None \u2192 auto-download curriculum_medium.yaml from HF
    nsample=15,                      # diffusion samples per window; \u2191 accuracy \u2191 latency
)
# results columns: Anomaly (0/1), MAE (float), plus all original columns
print(results[["Anomaly", "MAE"]].describe())

Inference CLI (from the repo root):

# Synthetic data \u2014 auto-downloads weights on first run
uv run python examples/quick_example.py

# Your own CSV
uv run python examples/quick_example.py \
  --model-path final_model.pth \
  --config-path curriculum_medium.yaml \
  --dataset-path /path/to/data.csv

Multi-GPU inference:

from sdk.inference_ad import inference_ad_tesseract2_mp

results = inference_ad_tesseract2_mp(
    data=df,
    model_path="final_model.pth",
    nsample=15,
    num_processes=None,   # auto-detect GPU count
    use_dpm_solver=True,  # 50-100\u00d7 speedup over 500-step diffusion
    dpm_steps=20,
)
# Falls back to single-GPU when only 1 GPU available.
# 4 GPUs \u00d7 50\u00d7 DPM-Solver \u2248 200\u00d7 vs single-GPU standard diffusion.

Fine-tuning on your own normal-behavior data: Train on anomaly-free windows from your domain. Filter out anomalies from the training CSV before fine-tuning \u2014 anomalies in the training set degrade the reconstruction baseline.

uv run python examples/finetune_example.py \
  --csv /path/to/normal_data.csv \
  --val-csv /path/to/val_data.csv \   # optional; otherwise --val-ratio splits --csv
  --pretrained-model final_model.pth \
  --epochs 20 --batch-size 16 --lr 1e-5 \
  --output-dir artifacts/finetune_my_data

# Run inference with a fine-tuned checkpoint:
results = perform_anomaly_analysis_with_diffusion(
    df=df,
    threshold_strategy="scs",
    model_path="artifacts/finetune_my_data/best_finetuned_model.pth",
    config_path="artifacts/finetune_my_data/finetune_config.yaml",
    nsample=15,
)

Data requirements: \u2265 100 rows (default window_length), all numeric columns, no NaN/\u00b1Inf. Drop timestamp, ID, and label columns before calling the API \u2014 the high-level helper does this automatically. Feature count is PCA-reduced to target_dim=18 if larger, or zero-padded if smaller.

Output files: inference writes examples/datasets/anomaly_results.csv (original columns + Anomaly + MAE). Fine-tuning writes best_finetuned_model.pth, final_finetuned_model.pth, metrics.json, and finetune_config.yaml under --output-dir.

Common pitfalls: HfHubHTTPError: 401 \u2192 set HUGGINGFACE_HUB_TOKEN or run huggingface-cli login. ValueError: No numeric columns \u2192 drop string/date columns before calling the API. ModuleNotFoundError: sdk \u2192 run from the ad_diffusion/ directory or add it to sys.path.

Data · Mining, Captioning, Annotation, SDG

Data how-tos cover dataset preparation, analysis, and augmentation — DAFT validation, k-NN mining, gap analysis, VLM captioning, synthetic anomaly generation, and Chain-of-Thought video annotation. All run through the TAO Execution SDK on the same managed backends as the model how-tos.

Reminder: every command = [...] below contains placeholder values for dataset paths and pipeline parameters. Ask the user for their real values before submitting — see How To Use This Guide.

How To Validate a TAO DAFT Dataset dataset validation

Run tao-daft validate to check NVIDIA TAO DAFT datasets for structure, schema, and cross-reference errors. Do not use for non-DAFT formats.

tao-daftdatasetvalidationschema
Step 0 — Confirm environment variables (before anything else in this flow — even after collecting the action inputs). Collect every action input from the user — never pick one yourself. Image: nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-data-services · Subcommand: tao-daft validate

<daft-format> is a positional subcommand slug. The two currently supported formats are metropolis-v3.0 (datasets containing a contextual/ directory alongside raw/ and task/) and cosmos-reason-v1.0 (datasets with a meta.json file alongside media/ and text/). Run tao-daft validate --help inside the container to list all formats installed in the current image version.
ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="tao-daft validate <daft-format> --path /data/dataset",
    inputs={"/data/dataset": "<daft-dataset-uri>"},
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-data-services",
    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=1,
)

How To Run the Cosmos AnomalyGen Pipeline synthetic data

Cosmos AnomalyGen runs as a single container image with three modes: fine-tune + generate (full), train only (finetune_only), or generate from an existing checkpoint (inference_only). The full pipeline produces synthetic anomaly images, evaluates them with nn_score, and searches per-sample (guidance, crop_ratio).

Running the Full Pipeline (finetune + generate + evaluate)

ep = build_entrypoint(

        "cosmos_anomalygen run --mode full"
        " --train-dataset /data/train"
        " --num-samples-per-class <num-samples-per-class>"
        " --output /results"

    outputs=["/results/"],
)

    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>,
)

Running in finetune_only Mode

Step 1 — Submit the finetune-only job.The job produces a fine-tuned checkpoint and exits; it does not run the generation phase.

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command=(
        "cosmos_anomalygen run --mode finetune_only"
        " --train-dataset /data/train"
        " --output /results"
    ),
    inputs={"/data/train": "<train-dataset-uri>"},
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nv-metropolis-dev/metropolis-sdg/cosmos-anomalygen:1.0.3-006434bb.main",
    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>,
)

Running in inference_only Mode (Generate from an Existing Checkpoint)

Step 1 — Submit the inference-only job.Run it with a previously fine-tuned checkpoint to generate synthetic anomaly images without retraining.

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command=(
        "cosmos_anomalygen run --mode inference_only"
        " --checkpoint /ckpt/model.pt"
        " --num-samples-per-class <num-samples-per-class>"
        " --output /results"
    ),
    inputs={"/ckpt/model.pt": "<checkpoint-uri>"},
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nv-metropolis-dev/metropolis-sdg/cosmos-anomalygen:1.0.3-006434bb.main",
    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>,
)

How To Auto-Label Image Phrase Grounding auto-label

Two-step image grounding pipeline: extracts referring expressions from (image, caption) pairs and grounds them to pixel-space bounding boxes via a VLM.

Image: nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-pyt · Subcommand: image_grounding run · Required env: GOOGLE_API_KEY (Gemini: vlm.backend: "gemini") or set vlm.backend: "openai" with vlm.openai.base_url, vlm.openai.model_name, and vlm.openai.api_key in the spec for any OpenAI-compatible endpoint (NIM, TAO inference microservice, vLLM)

Minimal spec YAML template (image_grounding) — click to expand 
# image_grounding spec — fill in the <container-path> values to match your inputs dict
images: /data/images            # container path — matches inputs["/data/images"] below
captions: /data/captions.jsonl  # container path — matches inputs["/data/captions.jsonl"] below
output: /results                # container path — matches outputs=["/results/"]
vlm:
  backend: "gemini"             # "gemini" (uses GOOGLE_API_KEY env var) or "openai"
  # If backend is "openai":
  # openai:
  #   base_url: "https://integrate.api.nvidia.com/v1"
  #   model_name: "<model-id>"
  #   api_key: "<api-key>"
batch_size: 10                  # images processed per VLM call; lower to reduce rate-limit errors
import os
# Edit an image-grounding spec YAML to point its `images`, `captions`, and
# `output` keys at the container paths below; upload it and reference at
# /specs/image_grounding.yaml.
ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="auto_label generate -e /specs/image_grounding.yaml",
    inputs={
        "/specs/image_grounding.yaml": "<edited-spec-uri>",
        "/data/images":                "<images-uri>",
        "/data/captions.jsonl":        "<captions-jsonl-uri>",
    },
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-pyt",
    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>,
    env_vars={"GOOGLE_API_KEY": os.environ.get("GOOGLE_API_KEY", "")},
)

Before executing any later step, ask the user to confirm that every required variable below has been set — expect a plain "yes" or "no, let me set them and restart." Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. Defer any actual environment check to the moment just before you run the code that consumes these variables. If any required variable is missing, stop here — have the user export it in the launching shell and restart the agent before continuing; do not set it mid-session, since later steps read it from the launching environment.
  • NGC_KEY — NGC API key (container image pulls from nvcr.io; get it at NGC →)
  • ACCESS_KEY + SECRET_KEY — S3-compatible credentials (only if input / output parquet URIs use s3://)
Also export credentials for your target compute backend — see the matching Platform how-to.

Image: nvcr.io/nvidian/iva/tao-toolkit-ds:aoi · Subcommand: deft_aoi_routing

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="deft_aoi_routing --weakest /data/weakest.parquet --out-knn /results/route_knn.parquet --out-anomalygen /results/route_anomalygen.parquet",
    inputs={"/data/weakest.parquet": "<weakest-parquet-uri>"},
    outputs=["/results/"],
)
sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nvidian/iva/tao-toolkit-ds:aoi",
    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=1,
)

How To Run VLM BCQ Gap Analysis gap analysis

Extract false-positive and false-negative failure cases from VLM binary-classification-question (BCQ, yes/no) predictions. Use as the DEFT root-cause analysis step after running a VLM on a binary evaluation task — the resulting FP/FN JSONL feeds downstream Cosmos generation or RCA stages.

gap-analysisvlmrccafalse-positivefalse-negativedeft
Step 0 — Confirm environment variables (before anything else in this flow — even after collecting the action inputs). Collect every action input from the user — never pick one yourself. This covers the model / checkpoint (model_path), gpu_count, num_nodes, the compute backend, and every other <…> placeholder: prompt the user for each before composing the command, and where the guide names a default or recommended value (or you could infer one from the hardware, e.g. reading nvidia-smi to size gpu_count), offer it as a suggestion in that prompt but do not adopt it until the user confirms. This action uses the variables listed below: required ones must already be exported in the shell that launched your agent; optional ones are only needed for the cases noted. Before executing any later step, ask the user to confirm that every required variable below has been set — expect a plain "yes" or "no, let me set them and restart." Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. Defer any actual environment check to the moment just before you run the code that consumes these variables. If any required variable is missing, stop here — have the user export it in the launching shell and restart the agent before continuing; do not set it mid-session, since later steps read it from the launching environment.
  • NGC_KEY — NGC API key (container image pulls from nvcr.io; get it at NGC →)
  • ACCESS_KEY + SECRET_KEY — S3-compatible credentials (only if predictions JSON or results URIs use s3://)
Also export credentials for your target compute backend — see the matching Platform how-to.

Image: nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-data-services · Subcommand: gap_analysis vlm_bcq

Input — predictions JSON format (one item per sample, each item must have video_id, response, and gt):

[
  {
    "video_id": "/path/to/video.mp4",
    "response": "Yes, there is a collision.",
    "gt": "B. No",
    "question": "Is there a collision?"
  },
  ...
]

Word-boundary matching is used for response and gt — "yes" or "no" anywhere in the string is recognised. Samples where both or neither are detected are skipped with a warning.

from tao_sdk.script_runner import build_entrypoint

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command=(
        "gap_analysis vlm_bcq"
        " predictions_json=/data/predictions.json"
        " results_dir=/results/gaps"
    ),
    inputs={
        "/data/predictions.json": "<predictions-json-uri>",  # JSON array from VLM evaluation run
    },
    outputs=["/results/gaps/"],
)
sdk.create_job(
    image="nvcr.io/nvidia/tao/tao-toolkit:7.0.0-data-services",
    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=0,   # CPU-only; no GPU required for gap analysis
)

Pass videos_dir when video_id values in the predictions are relative paths:

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command=(
        "gap_analysis vlm_bcq"
        " predictions_json=/data/predictions.json"
        " results_dir=/results/gaps"
        " videos_dir=/videos"
    ),
    inputs={
        "/data/predictions.json": "<predictions-json-uri>",
        "/videos":                "<videos-root-uri>",
    },
    outputs=["/results/gaps/"],
)

Outputs (written under results_dir):

  • kpi_gaps.jsonl — one JSON object per FP/FN case: video_id (absolute path), error_type (FP or FN), question, ground_truth, response. Pass this file to downstream Cosmos generation or RCA stages.
  • kpi_gaps_report.txt — human-readable table with total FP/FN counts. Surface these counts to the user after the run.

If no gaps are found, no files are written and a message is logged.

Common errors: FileNotFoundError — predictions_json path does not exist; check the URI. ValueError: must be a JSON array — wrap predictions in […]. ValueError: missing 'gt'/'response'/'video_id' — a prediction item is missing a required field; inspect and fix the JSON. Samples silently skipped — response or gt contains both or neither "yes"/"no"; check logs for warnings.

Platform · Where Jobs Run

Each platform is a managed compute backend the TAO Execution SDK can target. Pick one based on where your data sits and what scheduling guarantees you need. The SDK shape is identical across platforms — only the constructor and a handful of platform-specific create_job kwargs change.

Default recommendation — DockerSDK (local Docker). If you're developing on a local-GPU workstation, DockerSDK is the lowest-friction path: it targets the Docker daemon on the machine you're already on, with no cluster, instance lifecycle, or scheduler to provision. When an agent presents backend choices for any flow (training, inference microservice, AutoML, etc.), list DockerSDK (local Docker) first and mark it (Recommended); offer BrevSDK, SlurmSDK, and KubernetesSDK after, for when the workload outgrows a single local machine.

How To Run TAO on NVIDIA Brev (BrevSDK) managed gpu

NVIDIA Brev provides on-demand GPU instances pre-loaded with NVIDIA drivers, CUDA, and the container runtime. The BrevSDK wraps Brev's instance lifecycle (create, reuse, destroy) and the in-instance container launch so you submit jobs with the same create_job shape used for every other backend.

brevgpuinstance-based
Step 0 — Confirm environment variables (before anything else in this flow — even after collecting the action inputs). Collect every action input from the user — never pick one yourself. This covers the model / checkpoint (model_path), gpu_count, num_nodes, the compute backend, and every other <…> placeholder: prompt the user for each before composing the command, and where the guide names a default or recommended value (or you could infer one from the hardware, e.g. reading nvidia-smi to size gpu_count), offer it as a suggestion in that prompt but do not adopt it until the user confirms. This action uses the variables listed below: required ones must already be exported in the shell that launched your agent; optional ones are only needed for the cases noted. Before executing any later step, ask the user to confirm that every required variable below has been set — expect a plain "yes" or "no, let me set them and restart." Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. Defer any actual environment check to the moment just before you run the code that consumes these variables. If any required variable is missing, stop here — have the user export it in the launching shell and restart the agent before continuing; do not set it mid-session, since later steps read it from the launching environment.
  • BREV_API_TOKEN — Brev API token (authenticates BrevSDK to manage GPU instances)
  • NGC_KEY — optional; only if submitting jobs that pull nvcr.io container images
  • ACCESS_KEY + SECRET_KEY — optional; only if job inputs / outputs use s3:// URIs
  • HF_TOKEN — optional; only if jobs reference gated HuggingFace models

Required env: BREV_API_TOKEN. Optional: ACCESS_KEY, SECRET_KEY for S3 dataset/result wrapping.

import os
from tao_sdk.platforms.brev import BrevSDK

# Build the spec YAML and call build_entrypoint as shown in "The Common SDK
# Pattern" — pass ep["command"] below.

sdk = BrevSDK()           # auto-authenticates with BREV_API_TOKEN
job = sdk.create_job(
    image="<image>",                          # <-- copy from the chosen model entry's "Image" field
    command=ep["command"],                    # from build_entrypoint(...) — see Common SDK Pattern
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>,                    # single-instance only (1-8 GPUs); Brev has no num_nodes
    env_vars={"HF_TOKEN": os.environ["HF_TOKEN"]},
    # Brev-specific:
    instance_id="<instance-id>",              # reuse an existing instance; omit to create fresh
    gpu_type="<gpu-type>",                    # used when creating a new instance
    cloud_cred_id="<cloud-cred-id>",          # optional; required on multi-credential accounts (brev orgs --json)
    workspace_group_id="<workspace-group-id>", # optional; required on multi-workspace accounts (brev ls --json)
)
print(sdk.get_job_status(job.id))
Note: Multi-node is not supported on Brev — one job runs on one instance. Multi-GPU on a single instance (up to 8× H100 / A100 / L40S) works fine.

How To Run TAO on a SLURM Cluster (SlurmSDK) scheduler

Run TAO jobs on a remote SLURM GPU cluster over SSH with sbatch / srun, Pyxis/Enroot containers, and Lustre-backed results.

slurmsbatchpyxisenrootlustre
Step 0 — Confirm environment variables (before anything else in this flow — even after collecting the action inputs). Collect every action input from the user — never pick one yourself. This covers the model / checkpoint (model_path), gpu_count, num_nodes, the compute backend, and every other <…> placeholder: prompt the user for each before composing the command, and where the guide names a default or recommended value (or you could infer one from the hardware, e.g. reading nvidia-smi to size gpu_count), offer it as a suggestion in that prompt but do not adopt it until the user confirms. This action uses the variables listed below: required ones must already be exported in the shell that launched your agent; optional ones are only needed for the cases noted. Before executing any later step, ask the user to confirm that every required variable below has been set — expect a plain "yes" or "no, let me set them and restart." Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. Defer any actual environment check to the moment just before you run the code that consumes these variables. If any required variable is missing, stop here — have the user export it in the launching shell and restart the agent before continuing; do not set it mid-session, since later steps read it from the launching environment.
  • SLURM_HOST — SLURM login node hostname (required by SlurmSDK)
  • SLURM_USER — SLURM cluster username (required by SlurmSDK)
  • SSH_KEY_PATH — path to SSH private key for passwordless login (defaults to ~/.ssh/id_ed25519 if unset)
  • HF_TOKEN — optional; only if jobs reference gated HuggingFace models

Required env: SLURM_USER, SLURM_HOSTNAME. Optional: SSH_KEY_PATH (defaults to ~/.ssh/id_ed25519).

One-time host setup — passwordless SSH from your client to a SLURM login node (no secret stored in env, only the path to your existing private key):

ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -N "" -f ~/.ssh/id_ed25519
ssh-copy-id -i ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub "$SLURM_USER@<login-host>"
ssh-keyscan -H <login-host> >> ~/.ssh/known_hosts

# Verify — must succeed without any prompts
ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o PreferredAuthentications=publickey \
  "$SLURM_USER@<login-host>" 'hostname && squeue -u $USER -h | head -n 1'

Then submit jobs:

import os
from tao_sdk.platforms.slurm import SlurmSDK

# Build the spec YAML and call build_entrypoint as shown in "The Common SDK
# Pattern" — pass ep["command"] below. On SLURM, URIs in build_entrypoint's
# `inputs` are typically Lustre paths.

sdk = SlurmSDK()
job = sdk.create_job(
    image="<image>",                              # <-- copy from the chosen model entry's "Image" field
    command=ep["command"],                        # from build_entrypoint(...) — see Common SDK Pattern
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>, num_nodes=<num-nodes>,
    env_vars={"HF_TOKEN": os.environ["HF_TOKEN"]},
    # SLURM-specific (discover: ssh $SLURM_USER@$SLURM_HOST "sinfo -s" for partitions and "sacctmgr show user $USER" for accounts):
    partition="<partition>", account="<account>",
    mounts=["<host>:<container>"],               # optional; extra Pyxis container-mount strings
)

Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. import os
from tao_sdk.platforms.kubernetes import KubernetesSDK

# Build the spec YAML and call build_entrypoint as shown in "The Common SDK
# Pattern" — pass ep["command"] below.

sdk = KubernetesSDK()
job = sdk.create_job(
    image="<image>",                              # <-- copy from the chosen model entry's "Image" field
    command=ep["command"],                        # from build_entrypoint(...) — see Common SDK Pattern
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>, num_nodes=<num-nodes>,
    env_vars={"HF_TOKEN": os.environ["HF_TOKEN"]},
    # Kubernetes-specific (discover namespaces: kubectl get ns; GPU products: kubectl get nodes -L nvidia.com/gpu.product):
    namespace="<namespace>",
    node_selector={"nvidia.com/gpu.product": "<gpu-product>"},
    tolerations=[{"key": "nvidia.com/gpu", "operator": "Exists"}],
    image_pull_secret="<secret-name>",            # optional; required when pulling from nvcr.io or other private registry
)

How To Run TAO on Local Docker via the SDK (DockerSDK) local docker

The DockerSDK targets the Docker daemon on the machine you're running the SDK from. It's the right choice for single-host development, smoke runs against a small dataset, debugging a spec file before you push the job to a managed backend, or running on a workstation that already has a GPU. The submission shape is identical to every other backend — you never call docker yourself; the SDK constructs and supervises the container.

localdockersingle-hostdebug
Step 0 — Confirm environment variables (before anything else in this flow — even after collecting the action inputs). Collect every action input from the user — never pick one yourself. This covers the model / checkpoint (model_path), gpu_count, num_nodes, the compute backend, and every other <…> placeholder: prompt the user for each before composing the command, and where the guide names a default or recommended value (or you could infer one from the hardware, e.g. reading nvidia-smi to size gpu_count), offer it as a suggestion in that prompt but do not adopt it until the user confirms. This action uses the variables listed below: required ones must already be exported in the shell that launched your agent; optional ones are only needed for the cases noted. Before executing any later step, ask the user to confirm that every required variable below has been set — expect a plain "yes" or "no, let me set them and restart." Ask the user directly; do not probe your own shell to decide this, since the agent's tool shell may not mirror the shell that launched the agent and can report a false "unset" for variables the user has in fact exported. Defer any actual environment check to the moment just before you run the code that consumes these variables. If any required variable is missing, stop here — have the user export it in the launching shell and restart the agent before continuing; do not set it mid-session, since later steps read it from the launching environment.
  • NGC_KEY — NGC API key (required; DockerSDK uses it to authenticate docker pull from nvcr.io)
  • ACCESS_KEY + SECRET_KEY — optional; only if job inputs / outputs use s3:// URIs
  • HF_TOKEN — optional; only if jobs reference gated HuggingFace models

Required: Docker daemon reachable and able to schedule GPUs (the SDK relies on the container runtime supporting --gpus). Required env: NGC_KEY for nvcr.io pulls.

import os
from tao_sdk.platforms.docker import DockerSDK

# Build the spec YAML and call build_entrypoint as shown in "The Common SDK
# Pattern" — URIs passed to its `inputs` can be local paths, s3://, hf_model://,
# or ngc:// for any backend.

sdk = DockerSDK()
job = sdk.create_job(
    image="<image>",                              # <-- copy from the chosen model entry's "Image" field
    command=ep["command"],                        # from build_entrypoint(...) — see Common SDK Pattern
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>,                       # single host, single or multi-GPU
    env_vars={
        "NGC_KEY":  os.environ["NGC_KEY"],
        "HF_TOKEN": os.environ.get("HF_TOKEN", ""),
    },
    # DockerSDK-specific:
    mounts=[{"source": "<host-extra-mount-source>", "target": "<container-extra-mount-target>",
             "type": "bind", "read_only": True}],
    shm_size="8g",                                # optional; defaults to Docker's small /dev/shm — bump for PyTorch DataLoaders
)
print(sdk.get_job_status(job.id))
print(sdk.get_job_logs(job.id))
Same API as managed backends. Promoting a workload from DockerSDK to BrevSDK / SlurmSDK / KubernetesSDK is a one-line constructor change plus swapping local paths for the appropriate URIs (s3://, /lustre/...).

The TAO Execution SDK · API Surface sdk

One Python module per backend, all with the same five-method surface: create_job, get_job_status, get_job_logs, get_failure_analysis, cancel_job. Swapping platforms is one import change.

sdkjob-handless3multi-nodedistributed

Two-layer pattern. create_job(...) takes only image, command, gpu_count, env_vars, plus platform-specific kwargs — not inputs / outputs. Data-staging (S3/HF/NGC download, output upload, spec rewriting) is handled by tao_sdk.script_runner.build_entrypoint(...), which inlines the runtime into a self-contained shell command. You call build_entrypoint first, then pass its returned "command" to create_job.

import os
from tao_sdk.platforms.brev       import BrevSDK
from tao_sdk.platforms.slurm      import SlurmSDK
from tao_sdk.platforms.kubernetes import KubernetesSDK
from tao_sdk.platforms.docker     import DockerSDK
from tao_sdk.script_runner        import build_entrypoint

sdk = SlurmSDK()          # or BrevSDK(), KubernetesSDK(), DockerSDK()

# Step 1 — inline the data-staging runtime around your action command.
ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="<action-command>",                   # <-- from the chosen model entry (e.g. "dino train -e /specs/train.yaml")
    inputs={
        "/specs/train.yaml":              "<edited-spec-uri>",
        "/data/train/images.tar.gz":      "<train-images-uri>",
        # …one entry per file the spec references — see each model entry
    },
    outputs=["/results/"],
)

# Step 2 — submit the job. `command=ep["command"]` is the inlined launcher.
job = sdk.create_job(
    image="<image>",                              # <-- from the chosen model entry's "Image" field
    command=ep["command"],
    gpu_count=<gpu-count>, num_nodes=<num-nodes>,
    env_vars={"HF_TOKEN": os.environ["HF_TOKEN"]},
)
status   = sdk.get_job_status(job.id)
logs     = sdk.get_job_logs(job.id)
failure  = sdk.get_failure_analysis(job.id)         # structured RCA on failed jobs
sdk.cancel_job(job.id)

The Three Modes of build_entrypoint

build_entrypoint infers its mode from the kwargs you pass — exactly one of specs, args, or neither. Passing both specs and args raises ValueError.

passthrough — neither specs nor args. Use when the user has already uploaded a complete spec YAML (or has no spec at all) and you only need URI download + command exec + output upload. inputs is path-keyed ({container_path: uri}); command runs verbatim.

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="dino train -e /specs/train.yaml",
    inputs={
        "/specs/train.yaml":              "s3://bucket/specs/train.yaml",
        "/data/train/images.tar.gz":      "s3://bucket/data/imgs.tar.gz",
    },
    outputs=["/results/"],
)

config — pass specs={...}. Use when you want to build the spec as a Python dict instead of editing+uploading a YAML file. The runtime writes the dict to a file inside the container and substitutes the path into your command via {config_path}. URIs inside the spec at file-valued keys get downloaded; inputs declares which spec keys to fetch (dotted-path keys or a list), not container paths. config_format picks the on-disk format (toml default; also yaml, json).

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="dino train -e {config_path}",
    specs={
        "dataset": {
            "train_data_sources": [{
                "image_dir":  "s3://bucket/data/train/images/",     # downloaded by the runtime
                "json_file":  "s3://bucket/data/train/annotations.json",
            }],
            "val_data_sources": [{"image_dir": "s3://bucket/data/val/images/",
                                   "json_file": "s3://bucket/data/val/annotations.json"}],
        },
        "model": {"num_classes": 80},
        "train": {"num_epochs": 50, "results_dir": "/results"},
    },
    inputs=[                                       # spec-key paths to download (not container paths)
        "dataset.train_data_sources.image_dir",
        "dataset.train_data_sources.json_file",
        "dataset.val_data_sources.image_dir",
        "dataset.val_data_sources.json_file",
    ],
    outputs={"train.results_dir": {}},             # dict form — see "outputs= forms" below
    config_format="yaml",
)

args — pass args={...}. Use for scripts that take CLI args instead of a spec file. command contains {arg_name} placeholders that get substituted from the args dict; the runtime rewrites URI-valued args to local container paths before substituting. inputs declares which arg names hold input URIs (downloaded); outputs declares which arg names hold output URIs (uploaded after the command finishes). Boolean-style flags that aren't substituted go in flags=[...].

ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="knn_mining --target {target} --source-pool {source_pool} --output {output} --topk 50",
    args={
        "target":      "s3://bucket/weakest.parquet",        # input URI  → downloaded
        "source_pool": "s3://bucket/source_pool.parquet",    # input URI  → downloaded
        "output":      "s3://bucket/results/mined.parquet",  # output URI → uploaded after run
    },
    inputs=["target", "source_pool"],                        # arg names with input URIs
    outputs=["output"],                                      # arg names with output URIs
    flags=["--verbose"],
)

No I/O at all. If your command reads only from the container filesystem (data-services scripts with no external download/upload), skip build_entrypoint entirely and pass the raw shell string to create_job directly.

outputs= forms

outputs accepts either a list or a dict. Both forms key by the same thing — and what "the same thing" means depends on mode:

  • passthrough — keys are container paths (e.g. "/results/").
  • config — keys are dotted spec-key paths (e.g. "train.results_dir"). The runtime reads the container path from the spec value at that key.
  • args — keys are arg names (e.g. "output"). The runtime substitutes the matching {arg_name} placeholder and uploads the local path it wrote.

List form is shorthand for the dict form with empty options per key — ["k"] ≡ {"k": {}}. Reach for the dict form when you want per-output options (rare today, but it's the supported extension point).

# Equivalent in passthrough mode:
outputs=["/results/"]
outputs={"/results/": {}}

# Dict form lets you tune per-output behavior — e.g. exclude staging files:
ep = build_entrypoint(
    command="my_pipeline --out /results",
    inputs={"/data/in": "s3://bucket/data/in"},
    outputs={"/results/": {}},
    upload_excludes=["*.tmp", "staging/"],   # applied to every output path
)

The upload_excludes list is applied across all output paths and matches against paths relative to each output dir. Use it when an output dir contains files you don't want shipped to S3 (caches, intermediate artifacts).

Platform-specific create_job kwargs

Common platform-specific kwargs accepted by create_job:

Brev:       instance_id, gpu_type, cloud_cred_id, workspace_group_id  # single-instance, no num_nodes
SLURM:      num_nodes, partition, account, mounts
Kubernetes: num_nodes, namespace, node_selector, tolerations, image_pull_secret
Docker:     mounts, shm_size                                          # single-host

References · Placeholder Definitions

Every <…> placeholder that comes from this guide (rather than the user's dataset or compute entitlements) is defined here. Nothing in this section requires the LLM agent to read content from outside this document. The only external URLs that appear are account-side portals where a human user obtains credentials to export into their environment.

Placeholders defined in this guide lookup table

Definitions and valid value ranges for the <…> placeholders that come from this guide rather than from the user's own dataset / compute entitlements. Every value listed here is fully described inside this document — no external lookups are required.

Placeholder definitions — click to expand
Placeholder What it is
<network> The TAO model name — one of the model identifiers listed under Models · Train, Evaluate, Infer, Export. Examples: dino, clip, visual_changenet, cosmos_rl.
<action> A model action documented in the model's how-to entry. Most TAO models support train, evaluate, inference, export, and gen_trt_engine; not every action exists for every model.
<image> A container image tag from NVIDIA NGC. The exact tag to use is documented in each model's how-to entry under "Image"; the high-level map is in How To Choose the Right TAO Container Image.
<algorithm> An AutoML algorithm name. Valid values: bayesian, hyperband, asha, bohb, dehb, pbt, llm, hybrid, autoresearch. See How To Run AutoML.
<platform> The compute backend extra for pip install nvidia-tao-sdk[<platform>]. Valid values: brev, slurm, kubernetes, docker, all. See How To Install the TAO Execution SDK.
<config-format> The serialisation format of the model's spec file. Most TAO models use yaml; Cosmos-RL uses toml. The exact value is shown in each model's how-to entry.
<results-dir> The model's output spec key. For most TAO models this is results_dir; Cosmos-RL uses train.output_dir. The literal value is in each model's how-to entry.
<action-command> The exact shell command the action runs inside the container, with the spec file at a literal container path. Listed verbatim in each model's how-to entry — e.g. "dino train -e /specs/train.yaml".

Where users obtain credentials user-side only

This document does not require the LLM agent to visit any external resource. The URLs below exist solely so a human user can obtain account-side credentials (API keys, tokens) to export into the environment per How To Export All Required Environment Variables. The agent never needs to read these pages.

Credential portals by service — click to expand
  • NVIDIA NGC API key — for NGC_KEY. Account-side portal: ngc.nvidia.com.
  • HuggingFace user access token — for HF_TOKEN. Account-side token page: huggingface.co/settings/tokens.
  • Weights & Biases API key — for WANDB_API_KEY. Account-side page: wandb.ai/authorize.
  • NVIDIA Brev API token — for BREV_API_TOKEN. From the Brev console settings page.
  • S3-compatible storage keys — for ACCESS_KEY and SECRET_KEY. From the storage provider's account settings.
  • LLM-guided AutoML credentials — for AUTOML_LLM_API_KEY (or NVIDIA_API_KEY as fallback). Key and endpoint depend on the LLM provider you choose (e.g. NVIDIA Inference Microservices at inference-api.nvidia.com).
  • Google API key — for GOOGLE_API_KEY (Gemini VLM backend in image-grounding, referring-expression, and video-reasoning-annotation pipelines). From Google AI Studio or a Google Cloud project with the Generative Language API enabled.
